After some direct computations, we derive that

\displaystyle(\cos\theta)^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\left(\cos\theta U(\Phi)\right)+\alpha z^{-\frac{3}{\alpha}}D_{z}\left(z^{\frac{3}{\alpha}}V(\Phi)\right)=0.

This implies that

\displaystyle T_{f}

\displaystyle=(\cos\theta)^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\left(\cos\theta U(\Phi)f\right)+\alpha z^{-\frac{3}{\alpha}}D_{z}\left(z^{\frac{3}{\alpha}}V(\Phi)f\right)

\displaystyle~~~~-\left((\cos\theta)^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\left(cos\theta U(\Phi)\right)+\alpha z^{-\frac{3}{\alpha}}D_{z}\left(z^{\frac{3}{\alpha}}V(\Phi)\right)\right)f

\displaystyle=(\cos\theta)^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\left(\cos\theta U(\Phi)f\right)+\alpha z^{-\frac{3}{\alpha}}D_{z}\left(z^{\frac{3}{\alpha}}V(\Phi)f\right).

Hence, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\big\langle T_{f},fz^{\frac{3}{\alpha}-1}\cos\theta\big\rangle=0.

(4.33)

Assume that
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}(z,\theta)
is the weight for establishing the
L^{2}
stability of
g
, then the weight
\mathcal{W}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\triangleq\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}~z^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2\alpha}}(\cos\theta)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(4.34)

which, along with (
4.33
), yields that

\displaystyle\big\langle\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}}T_{g\mathcal{W}},g(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}})^{2}\big\rangle=\big\langle T_{g\mathcal{W}},\big(g\mathcal{W}\big)z^{\frac{3}{\alpha}-1}\cos\theta\big\rangle=0.

(4.35)

For more details, one can refer to Lemma
7.4
in Section
7
.

(2.2) Analysis of the homogeneous part

Recalling (
4.10
), we treat
\Phi
as the perturbation of
G^{\ast}_{F^{\ast}_{\gamma}}\sin(2\theta)
. With the help of (
3.18
), (
4.27
) and (
4.28
), we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}}T_{\mathcal{W}}\cdot g

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{3}{2}\frac{D_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}+\alpha(\cos(2\theta)-(\sin\theta)^{2})\frac{D_{z}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}\right)L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})~g+\text{Nonlinear ~perturbation}.

(4.36)

We assume that weight
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
satisfies the form of variable separation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\triangleq\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}~\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta},~~~\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}\in C(0,\infty),~~~\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}\in C\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right).

(4.37)

According to the detailed analysis of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}
, we take
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}=w_{z}
and apply (
4.34
), to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{D_{z}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}=\frac{D_{z}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}}{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{z}}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{2\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}).

When
\alpha
is small enough,
\frac{3}{2\alpha}
occupies the majority in
\frac{D_{z}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}
.
Thus, one can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{W}}T_{\mathcal{W}}\cdot g

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{D_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}+(\cos(2\theta)(\sin\theta)^{2})\right)L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})~g+\text{Linear/nonlinear ~perturbation}.

In order to treat the homogeneous part to be a perturbation of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}
, we roughly require that

\displaystyle\frac{D_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}+\cos(2\theta)-(\sin\theta)^{2}=0.

This, together with (
4.34
), (
4.37
) and

\frac{D_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}=\frac{D_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}}{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}}+(\sin\theta)^{2},

implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}}{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\sin\theta}{\cos\theta}-\frac{\cos\theta}{\sin\theta}\right).

Here, we solve the equation above to obtain that
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\theta}=(\sin(2\theta))^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
If we take the weight
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}=w_{z}~(\sin(2\theta))^{-\xi}
,
then the following identity can be obtained

\displaystyle\frac{D_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}+\cos(2\theta)-(\sin\theta)^{2}=\frac{D_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}}+\cos(2\theta)=(1-2\xi)\cos(2\theta).

(4.38)

Note that the homogeneous part or even entire transport term
T
acts as the perturbation of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}
if
\xi
is approximately equal to
\frac{1}{2}
.
Thus, we identify the structure (
4.35
) and the identity (
4.38
) with
\xi=\frac{1}{2}
as the null structure of transport term
T
.
In view of (
4.32
), (
4.35
), (
4.36
) and (
4.38
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle T_{g}=\text{Null~perturbation}+\text{Nonlinear~perturbation}.

(4.39)

(3) Conclusion

In the end, we conclude from (
4.14
), (
4.15
), (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.31
) and (
4.39
) that

\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g)-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta}=\text{Linear/nonlinear~perturbation}+\text{Null~perturbation}=\tilde{o}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}).

4.4.  Derivation of the self-similar system

In this subsection, we derive the equation of
\mu
, which ensures
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0
. To start with, we apply
L^{-1}_{z,K}
to self-similar system (
4.14
) and consider the value at
z=0
, to get that

\displaystyle L^{-1}_{z,K}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g)\big)(0)=-L^{-1}_{z,K}(T)(0)+L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{0})(0)+L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{1})(0)+L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{2})(0).

Integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle L^{-1}_{z,K}(D_{z}g)(0)=0,~~~L^{-1}_{z,K}\big(L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\gamma})g\big)(0)=-L^{-1}_{z,K}\big(L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\big)(0),

which, along with (
1.35
) and (
1.36
), implies that

\displaystyle L^{-1}_{z,K}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g)\big)(0)=L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0),~~~L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{1})(0)=-\mu L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0).

Thanks to (
3.18
) and (
4.24
), we see that

\displaystyle L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{0})(0)=-2\mu\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{\frac{1}{\gamma}-1}}{1+z^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}}\langle F^{\ast}_{\gamma},K\rangle_{\theta}dz=-\frac{8}{3}\frac{\alpha\mu}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{\frac{2}{\gamma}-1}}{(1+z^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})^{3}}dz=-\frac{4\alpha\mu}{3}.

Then, we conclude from the three estimates above that

\displaystyle(1+\mu)L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)

\displaystyle=L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{2}-T)(0)-\frac{4\alpha\mu}{3}.

(4.40)

If we take
\mu
satisfy

\displaystyle\mu=\frac{3}{4\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{2}-T)(0),

then (
4.40
) can be simplified as

\displaystyle(1+\mu)L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0.

Since
|\mu|<1
, which will be proven in Section
8
, it follows that

\displaystyle L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0.

This is consistent with (
4.19
) and completes the analysis of
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
eventually. In summary, we rewrite (
4
) as the following self-similar system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g)=-T+R_{0}+R_{1}+R_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
L^{\alpha}_{z}(\Phi)+L_{\theta}(\Phi)=F,\\[4.30554pt]
\mu=\frac{3}{4\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(R_{2}-T)(0),~~\gamma=\frac{1+\mu}{1-\mu}\beta,\\[4.30554pt]
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0,~~g|_{\partial D}=0,~~\Phi|_{\partial D}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
F=F^{\ast}_{\gamma}+g
, the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
and the remaining terms
R_{i}

(i=0,1,2)
is defined in (
1.35
)-(
1.37
), respectively.
Guided by the detailed analysis in this section, we will establish the global estimate of the self-similar system (
4.4
) in the remaining sections.
This, in turn, will enable us to ultimately establish the global existence of the self-similar system (
4
) with critical regularity.

5
Coercivity

Our goal in this section is to explore the coercivity of operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
.
Firstly, for any function
f
, we define the main operator
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(f)\triangleq\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(f)-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(f)F^{\ast}_{\beta},

(5.1)

where the core operator
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(f)\triangleq f+\beta D_{z}f-L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})f.

(5.2)

Recalling the definition of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
in (
1.35
),
then
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
can be divided into the following two parts, namely, the main operator and the perturbation term:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(f)=\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(f)+\tilde{P}(f),

(5.3)

where perturbation term
\tilde{P}(f)
is defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{P}(f)\triangleq L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})f+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(f)(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}).

(5.4)

Next, we recall the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
-norm.
For any
\beta\in(0,1]
, we remember that

\displaystyle\|f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}=

\displaystyle\frac{2-\beta}{2}\|fw^{K}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1-\beta}{2c_{\ast,\beta}\beta}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(f)w_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{z}}+\frac{1}{2c_{\ast,\beta}\beta}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(f)w_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{z}}.

(5.5)

We then introduce the
\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}([0,\infty)\times[0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
norm related to
\eta
as:

\displaystyle\|f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}\triangleq

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{2}(\|fw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|D_{z}fw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|D_{z}D_{\theta}fw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})+(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}fw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\|D^{2}_{\theta}fw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}fw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, we introduce the
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}([0,\infty)\times[0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
norm related to
\eta
as:

\displaystyle\|f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}}\triangleq\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|fw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}fw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(5.7)

Here, the definitions of the weights
w^{K}
,
w_{z}
,
w^{\eta}
and
w^{\lambda}
can be found in Section
2
.
Specifically, the key distinction between
w^{\lambda}
and
w^{\eta}
is their difference in angular weight, with
w^{\lambda}
exhibiting higher angular singularity.
Actually,
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
can be viewed as a complement to the
\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
norm, introducing extra angular weighting for radial derivatives.
While
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}}
itself does not constitute a “good” norm, the intersection space
\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}\cap\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
is equipped with a “good” norm.
Furthermore, the notations
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}
and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}
denote the corresponding inner products.

5.1.  Coercivity of main operator

Firstly, we give the estimate of the core operator
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}
, which is defined in (
5.2
).

Lemma 5.1
.

For any
\beta\in(0,1]
, there holds

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g),g(w_{z})^{2}\rangle_{z}=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)\|gw_{z}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{z}}.

(5.8)

Proof.

Recalling the natural radial weight
w_{z}(z)=2\beta^{-1}z^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{-1}
, we derive that

\displaystyle\frac{D_{z}w_{z}}{w_{z}}=-\left(\frac{D_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}+\frac{1}{\beta}\right).

(5.9)

This equality, when combined with (
3.6
), yields that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g)w_{z}

\displaystyle=gw_{z}+\beta D_{z}gw_{z}-L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})gw_{z}

\displaystyle=\beta D_{z}(gw_{z})+\left(1-\beta\frac{D_{z}w_{z}}{w_{z}}-L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})\right)gw_{z}

\displaystyle=\beta D_{z}(gw_{z})+\left(1+\beta\left(\frac{D_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}+\frac{1}{\beta}\right)-L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})\right)gw_{z}

\displaystyle=\beta D_{z}(gw_{z})+gw_{z}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g),g(w_{z})^{2}\rangle_{z}

\displaystyle=\langle\beta D_{z}(gw_{z})+gw_{z},gw_{z}\rangle_{z}=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)\|gw_{z}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{z}}.

Thus, we complete the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Based on the estimate of the core operator in Lemma
5.1
, we then investigate the coercivity of the main operator
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
, which is defined in (
5.1
).

Proposition 5.2
.

If
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0
, then there holds

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle=\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.10)

Moreover, there exist constants
\alpha>0
sufficiently small and
\eta(\beta)
, such that if
\alpha\ll 1-\eta\ll\beta
and
|\mu|\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{1}{100}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}},

(5.11)

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{1}{100}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}-\frac{1}{100}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}.

(5.12)

Proof.

According to the definitions of different norms, we will divide into the following three steps to prove (
5.10
), (
5.11
) and (
5.12
), respectively.

Step 1: Estimate for
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
-norm.

It follows from (
3.6
) and (
5.9
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(z\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}+D_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}+2\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}D_{z}w_{z}=\left(1-\frac{2}{\beta}\right)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}-D_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}

(5.13)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}.

Recall that the natural angular weight
w^{K}_{\theta}(\theta)=[K(\theta)]^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{\alpha}{3\beta})}
and
w^{K}(z,\theta)=w^{K}_{\theta}w_{z}
.
By virtue of
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0
, we can deduce from (
5.1
) that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle-\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta},g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle.

(5.14)

It follows from (
3.18
), (
5.1
), (
5.9
) and (
5.13
) that

\displaystyle\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta},g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle=\frac{1}{c_{\ast,\beta}}\langle L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},gKw_{z}^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{c_{\ast,\beta}}\langle L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{z}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}^{2}\rangle_{z}=\frac{1}{2c_{\ast,\beta}}\langle[L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)]^{2},\partial_{z}(z\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2})\rangle_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2c_{\ast,\beta}}\langle[L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)]^{2},\left(1-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}\rangle_{z}.

Substituting the estimate above and (
5.8
) into (
5.14
), we obtain (
5.10
) directly.

Step 2: Estimate for
\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm.

Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}=

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+\left[(1-\eta)^{-2}\langle D_{\theta}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D_{\theta}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right.

(5.15)

\displaystyle\left.+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle D_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]+\left[\langle D_{\theta}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D_{\theta}^{2}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle D_{\theta}D_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle D_{z}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D_{z}^{2}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}.

We note that
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
are related to the
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
,
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\eta}
and
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
norms, respectively.

Step 2.1: Estimate for
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm.

Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
in (
5.1
), we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g),g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle-(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta},g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\triangleq J_{11}+J_{12}.

(5.16)

By virtue of Lemma
5.1
, we infer that

\displaystyle J_{11}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(5.17)

In view of (
3.18
), one can get
that

\displaystyle|J_{12}|\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}w_{\theta}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\beta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}{L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}w_{\theta}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}},

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\|\frac{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}{L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\beta^{-1},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}w_{\theta}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin^{-\eta}(2\theta)d\theta\lesssim(1-\eta)^{-1},\quad\forall~\eta\in(0,1).

(5.20)

The combination of (
5.16
)-(
5.18
) gives that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.21)

Step 2.2: Estimate for
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\eta}
-norm.

After some calculations, one obtains that

\displaystyle D_{\theta}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{\theta}g)-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},

(5.22)

\displaystyle D_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{z}g)+\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}g-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}.

(5.23)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}=

\displaystyle(1-\eta)^{-2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{\theta}g),D_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{z}g),D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

(5.24)

\displaystyle-(1-\eta)^{-2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}g,D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle-(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{6}J_{2i}.

Next, we estimate
J_{2i}

(i=1,\cdots,6)
term by term.
By virtue of Lemma
5.1
, we infer that

\displaystyle J_{21}=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

(5.25)

\displaystyle J_{22}=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(5.26)

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle D_{\theta}\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha}{3\beta}\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}(\cos^{2}\theta-2\sin^{2}\theta),

(5.27)

which, together with (
5.19
) and Proposition
3.2
, gives that

\displaystyle|J_{23}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\beta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}}{L_{z}^{-1}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})}\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}(1-\eta)^{-1}\left((1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right),

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin^{-1+\frac{17\alpha}{30\beta}}(2\theta)d\theta\lesssim\frac{\beta}{\alpha}.

(5.29)

With the help of (
5.13
), we get that for any
0<\beta\leq 1
,

\displaystyle J_{24}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{z}g^{2}(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle=-\frac{1}{2}(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\partial_{z}(z\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}^{2}),g^{2}(w_{\theta}^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

(5.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\beta}{2\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq 0.

It follows from (
3.6
) that

\displaystyle D_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}=\frac{1}{\beta}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}\left(1-\frac{4z^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{1+z^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}\right).

(5.31)

This, together with (
5.19
), (
5.20
) and Proposition
3.2
, yields that

\displaystyle|J_{25}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.32)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}w_{\theta}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}\left((1-\eta)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

Thanks to (
3.15
) and Proposition
3.2
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle|J_{26}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}w_{\theta}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}\left((1-\eta)^{\frac{5}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

Substituting (
5.25
)-(
5.28
), (
5.30
)-(
5.33
) into (
5.24
), we conclude that

\displaystyle J_{2}\geq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}(1-\eta)^{-1}\right)(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}(1-\eta)^{-1}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

(5.34)

\displaystyle+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}\right)(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Step 2.3: Estimate for
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm.

Straightforward computations give that

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\theta}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D^{2}_{\theta}g)-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},

\displaystyle D_{\theta}D_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{\theta}D_{z}g)+\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}D_{\theta}g-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},

and

\displaystyle D^{2}_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D^{2}_{z}g)+2\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}D_{z}g+gD_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}+\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}

(5.35)

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}.

Applying three equalities above, we find that

\displaystyle J_{3}=

\displaystyle\left[\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D^{2}_{\theta}g),D^{2}_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D_{\theta}D_{z}g),D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right.

(5.36)

\displaystyle\left.+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D^{2}_{z}g),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}D_{\theta}g,D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle 2\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}D_{z}g,D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle-\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle-(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle-(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle gD_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\left[(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{9}J_{3i}.

Now, we begin to estimate
J_{3i}

(i=1,\cdots,9)
term by term.
Again thanks to Lemma
5.1
, one gets that

\displaystyle J_{31}=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)\left(\|D_{\theta}^{2}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}^{2}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

It then follows from a similar way as (
5.30
) that for any
0<\beta\leq 1
,

\displaystyle J_{32}=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta}{2\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq 0,

\displaystyle J_{33}=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\beta}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq 0.

Since

\displaystyle D_{\theta}^{2}\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}=\left(\frac{2\alpha}{3\beta}\right)^{2}\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}(\cos^{2}\theta-2\sin^{2}\theta)^{2}-\frac{2\alpha}{\beta}\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}\sin^{2}(2\theta),

we then deduce from a similar way as (
5.28
) that

\displaystyle|J_{34}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}\left(\|D^{2}_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

(5.37)

In the same manner as (
5.28
) and (
5.32
), one gets, by (
5.27
), (
5.29
) and (
5.31
), that

\displaystyle|J_{35}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\left((1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

A direct computation gives

\displaystyle D_{z}^{2}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}=\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}\left[\left(1-\frac{4z^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{1+z^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}\right)^{2}-\frac{4z^{\frac{1}{\beta}}}{(1+z^{\frac{1}{\beta}})^{2}}\right].

Combining this with (
5.20
) and applying a derivation analogous to (
5.32
), we obtain that

\displaystyle|J_{36}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{\frac{7}{2}}\|D_{z}^{2}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left((1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}^{2}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

(5.38)

Recalling (
5.27
), it then follows from a similar way as (
5.33
) that

\displaystyle|J_{37}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\lesssim\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}((1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}).

(5.39)

Using Proposition
3.2
and (
5.31
), we infer that

\displaystyle|J_{38}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|gD_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)\left((1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

Applying (
5.31
), we then deduce through a method analogous to (
5.33
) that

\displaystyle|J_{39}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{\frac{7}{2}}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}(\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}})

(5.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}((1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}).

Inserting
the estimates of
J_{3i}
with
i=1,\cdots,9
into (
5.36
), we have

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}\right)\|D_{\theta}^{2}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

(5.42)

\displaystyle+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle+\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\right)(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\eta}}).

We substitute (
5.21
) in
Step 2.1
, (
5.34
) in
Step 2.2
and (
5.42
) in
Step 2.3
into (
5.15
), and choose the suitable constants
\alpha
and
1-\eta
sufficiently small relative to
\beta
, to conclude that (
5.11
) holds.

Step 3: Estimate for
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm.

We recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
, to discover that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}=

\displaystyle\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle D^{2}_{z}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\triangleq K_{1}+K_{2},

(5.43)

where

K_{1}
is related to the
\mathcal{L}^{2}
norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
, while
K_{2}
is related to the radial
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
.

Step 3.1: Estimate for
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
.

According to the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
in (
5.1
),
K_{1}
can be divided into the following two terms:

\displaystyle K_{1}=\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(g),g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle-\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta},g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\triangleq K_{11}+K_{12}.

(5.44)

By virtue of Lemma
5.1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle K_{11}=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(5.45)

With the help of (
5.29
), one can infer that

\displaystyle|K_{12}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)(\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}).

Plugging (
5.45
) and (
5.46
) in (
5.44
), we conclude that

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C(1-\eta)\right)\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C(1-\eta)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.47)

Step 3.2: Estimate for the radial
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
.

Recalling (
5.35
) and the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
in (
5.1
), we divide
K_{2}
into the following five terms:

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}(D^{2}_{z}g),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle 2\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}D_{z}g,D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

(5.48)

\displaystyle~~~+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle gD_{z}\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle-\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle~~~+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\left(\langle\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta},D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{5}K_{2i}.

We now estimate
K_{2i}

(i=1,\cdots,5)
term by term.
Again thanks to Lemma
5.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle K_{21}=\left(1-\frac{\beta}{2}\right)\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(5.49)

We use a similar derivation of (
5.30
), to deduce that

\displaystyle K_{22}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\beta)\alpha}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\alpha}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D_{z}g(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}))^{\frac{1}{2}}w^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\geq 0.

(5.50)

A similar argument as (
5.40
) yields that

\displaystyle|K_{23}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.51)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1-\eta}{\beta^{2}}\left(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

In view of (
5.29
), we then deduce from a similar way as (
5.38
) that

\displaystyle|K_{24}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\|\frac{\Gamma_{\theta,\beta}}{c_{\ast,\beta}}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1-\eta}{\beta^{2}}\left(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

We employ (
5.29
) again, then use a similar derivation as (
5.41
), to find that

\displaystyle|K_{25}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}(\|\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}})

(5.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}(\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1-\eta}{\beta^{2}}(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}).

We substitute (
5.49
)-(
5.53
) into (
5.48
), to conclude that

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{\beta}{2}-C\frac{1-\eta}{\beta^{2}}\Big)\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-C\frac{1-\eta}{\beta^{2}}(\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\eta}}).

(5.54)

Inserting (
5.47
) in
Step 3.1
and (
5.54
) in
Step 3.2
into (
5.43
), choosing
1-\eta
sufficiently small relative to
\frac{1}{\beta}
, one can obtain (
5.12
) directly.
∎

5.2.  Parameter stability of main operator

With the coercivity of the main operator
\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}
shown in Proposition
5.2
at hand, our goal in this subsection is to establish the coercivity of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
.
We note that the parameter stability plays a crucial role in this analysis.
To begin with, we introduce the following Hardy-type inequality.

Lemma 5.3
.

If
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0
, then there holds

\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\leq C\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

Notice that

w_{z}^{2}=\frac{(1+z^{\frac{1}{\beta}})^{4}}{z^{\frac{4}{\beta}}}\approx 1+z^{-\frac{4}{\beta}},

hence, for any
k\neq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{-k}\left(L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\right)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|k-1|}\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}z^{1-k}\left(L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\right)^{2}dz\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|k-1|}\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{-k}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}g(z,\theta)K(\theta)d\theta dz\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{|k-1|}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{-k}\left(L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\right)^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}z^{-k}g^{2}(w_{\theta}^{K})^{2}d\theta dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}z^{-k}\left(L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)\right)^{2}dz\lesssim\frac{1}{|k-1|^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}z^{-k}g^{2}(w_{\theta}^{K})^{2}d\theta dz.

By choosing
k=0
and
k=\frac{4}{\beta}
, we thus complete the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

Building upon Lemma
5.3
, we now proceed to establish the coercivity of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)(0)=0
. There exist constants
\alpha>0
sufficiently small and
\eta(\beta)
, such that if
\alpha\ll 1-\eta\ll\beta
and
|\mu|\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}
, then

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{4}(\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}),

(5.55)

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{4}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{1}{50}(\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}).

(5.56)

Proof.

Recalling (
4.23
) and employing
|\mu|\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right|\leq\frac{2\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\beta}\ll 1.

Hence, for any
\delta_{0}>0
, we can choose
\alpha
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right|\leq\min\left\{\frac{\ln(1-\delta_{0})}{2\ln(\delta_{0})},\delta_{0}\right\}.

(5.57)

By Proposition
3.2
, we can obtain that (
3.20
) and (
3.24
) hold for parameters
\beta
and
\gamma
.
Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta},

(5.58)

\displaystyle\|L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})-L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\delta_{0}.

(5.59)

Then this proof will be divided into the following two steps.

Step 1: Estimate for
\mathcal{H}^{-1}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
-norm.

Recalling (
5.3
) and the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
, we find that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}

(5.60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}^{\beta}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle+\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}^{\beta}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}\right)+\langle\tilde{P}(g),g(w^{K})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\tilde{P}(g),g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle+\left[(1-\eta)^{-2}\langle D_{\theta}\tilde{P}(g),D_{\theta}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle D_{z}\tilde{P}(g),D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\langle D_{\theta}^{2}\tilde{P}(g),D_{\theta}^{2}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle+(1-\eta)^{2}\langle D_{\theta}D_{z}\tilde{P}(g),D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(1-\eta)^{4}\langle D_{z}^{2}\tilde{P}(g),D_{z}^{2}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{5}L_{i}.

Here
L_{1}
is the
\mathcal{H}^{-1}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
norm of the main operator, while
L_{2}
,
L_{3}
,
L_{4}
and
L_{5}
are the
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}^{2}
,
\dot{\mathcal{H}}_{\eta}^{1}
and
\dot{\mathcal{H}}_{\eta}^{2}
norms of the perturbation term, respectively.

Step 1.1: Estimate for
\mathcal{H}^{-1}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}
-norm of the main operator.

According to (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) in Proposition
5.2
, we compute that

\displaystyle L_{1}\geq\frac{1}{3}(\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}).

(5.61)

Step 1.2: Estimate for
\mathcal{H}^{-1}
-norm of the perturbation term.

In view of (
5.4
), (
5.58
), (
5.59
) and Lemma
5.3
, we find that

\displaystyle|L_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})-L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.62)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{0}\left(1+\frac{1}{\beta}\right)\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

Step 1.3: Estimate for
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm of the perturbation term.

We infer from (
5.4
), (
5.20
), (
5.58
), (
5.59
) and Lemma
5.3
that

\displaystyle|L_{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.63)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Step 1.4: Estimate for
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{\eta}
-norm of the perturbation term.

After some direct calculations, one obtains that

D_{\theta}\tilde{P}(g)=L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),

and

D_{z}\tilde{P}(g)=L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{z}g-(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}).

These give that

\displaystyle L_{4}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)^{-2}\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

(5.64)

\displaystyle~~~+(1-\eta)^{2}\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{z}g-(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})g,D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle~~~+(1-\eta)^{2}\left[\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{3}L_{4i}.

Next,
L_{4i}

(i=1,2,3)
will be controlled term by term.
In order to control the first term
L_{41}
, we apply (
5.58
), (
5.59
), Proposition
3.2
and Lemma
5.3
, to discover that

\displaystyle|L_{41}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta})-L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

(5.65)

\displaystyle~~~\left.+\left(\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w^{\lambda}_{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}w^{\lambda}_{\theta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{0}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\delta_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}\right)(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Taking advantage of (
5.58
), (
5.59
), Proposition
3.2
and Lemma
5.3
again, we achieve that

\displaystyle|L_{42}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{0}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

With the help of (
3.2
), (
5.57
)-(
5.59
), one gets that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}-D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right|\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}

(5.67)

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{\gamma}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}},

which, together with (
5.20
), (
5.58
) and Lemma
5.3
, implies that

\displaystyle|L_{43}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}-D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

(5.68)

\displaystyle~~~\left.+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}w^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Substituting (
5.65
)-(
5.68
) into (
5.64
), we end up with

\displaystyle|L_{4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\delta_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}\right)(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(5.69)

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}(1-\eta)^{-2}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}(1-\eta)\right)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Step 1.5: Estimate for
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm of the perturbation term.

Straightforward computations immediately show that

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\theta}\tilde{P}(g)

\displaystyle=L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D^{2}_{\theta}g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),

\displaystyle D_{\theta}D_{z}\tilde{P}(g)

\displaystyle=L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}D_{z}g-(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})

\displaystyle~~~-\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),

\displaystyle D^{2}_{z}\tilde{P}(g)

\displaystyle=L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D^{2}_{z}g-2(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{z}g-D_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})

(5.70)

\displaystyle~~~-\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}))-\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),

which implies that

\displaystyle L_{5}=

\displaystyle\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D^{2}_{\theta}g+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D^{2}_{\theta}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

(5.71)

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{2}\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}D_{z}g-(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{\theta}g,D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{2}\left[\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D_{\theta}D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D_{\theta}D_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{4}\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D^{2}_{z}g-2(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{z}g-D_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})g,D_{z}^{2}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\eta)^{4}\left[\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle-(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\eta})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{6}L_{5i}.

Now, we deal with
L_{5i}

(i=1,\cdots,6)
in turn.
Bounding the first term
L_{51}
by a similar way as (
5.65
), we thus get that

\displaystyle|L_{51}|\lesssim\left(\delta_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}\right)\|D^{2}_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.72)

For the term
L_{52}
, we apply an analogous approach to (
5.66
) and deduce that

\displaystyle|L_{52}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

(5.73)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{-2}\|D_{\theta}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

We employ (
5.29
), Lemma
5.3
and Proposition
3.2
, to find that

\displaystyle|L_{53}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\frac{3}{2\alpha}\left(\|D_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|D_{\theta}D_{z}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right.

(5.74)

\displaystyle~~~\left.+\frac{3}{2\alpha}\left(\|D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\beta}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|D_{\theta}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}w_{\theta}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{5}}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{3}}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{5}}}\left((1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{\theta}D_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

Along the similar line as (
5.66
), it is enough to prove that

\displaystyle|L_{54}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\delta_{0}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\|gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.75)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\left((1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

The combination of (
3.2
), (
5.57
)-(
5.59
) gives that

\displaystyle~~~~\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\beta}-D^{2}_{z}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\gamma}\right|\|\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta},K\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|\langle F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma},K\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{\gamma}\|\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta},K\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left|\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\right|\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}^{2}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})+\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-2\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\|\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(F^{\ast}_{\beta})-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}}^{2}\frac{3}{2\alpha}\|F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}.

This, combined with (
5.67
) and following a derivation analogous to (
5.68
), yields

\displaystyle|L_{55}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\eta)^{\frac{7}{2}}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.76)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\left((1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{3}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

It then follows from a similar way as (
5.68
) that

\displaystyle|L_{56}|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{\frac{7}{2}}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\left((1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{3}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

(5.77)

We then substitute (
5.72
)-(
5.75
), (
5.76
) and (
5.77
) into (
5.71
), to find that

\displaystyle|L_{5}|\lesssim\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{5}}}\right)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\eta}}+\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}+\sqrt{\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{5}}}\right)\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.78)

Inserting (
5.61
) in
Step 1.1
, (
5.62
) in
Step 1.2
, (
5.63
) in
Step 1.3
, (
5.69
) in
Step 1.4
and (
5.78
) in
Step 1.5
into (
5.60
), choosing the suitable transitional constant
\eta
,
\delta_{0}
, and taking
\alpha
small enough, we can conclude that (
5.55
) holds.

Step 2: Estimate for
\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}
-norm.

Recalling the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
, we find that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}^{\beta}_{\Gamma}(g),g\rangle_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}+\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\langle\tilde{P}(g),g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle D^{2}_{z}\tilde{P}(g),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

(5.79)

\displaystyle\triangleq M_{1}+M_{2}+M_{3},

where
M_{1}
is the
\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}
norm of the main operator, while
M_{2}
and
M_{3}
are the
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
and the radial
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
norms with the weight
w^{\lambda}
of the perturbation term, respectively.

Step 2.1: Estimate for
\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}
-norm of the main operator.

By (
5.12
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle M_{1}\geq\frac{1}{3}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{E}_{\eta}^{2}}-\frac{1}{100}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}-\frac{1}{100}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}.

(5.80)

Step 2.2: Estimate for
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
of the perturbation term.

We infer from (
5.58
), (
5.59
) and Lemma
5.3
that

\displaystyle|M_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}+\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)[K(\theta)]^{\frac{\alpha}{10\beta}}w^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.81)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\delta_{0}(1-\eta)^{2}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

Step 2.3: Estimate for the radial
\dot{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\eta}
-norm with the weight
w^{\lambda}
of the perturbation term.

Recalling (
5.70
),
M_{3}
can be writen as follows:

\displaystyle M_{3}

\displaystyle=\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\left[\langle L^{-1}_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D^{2}_{z}g-2(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})D_{z}g-D_{z}(\Gamma^{\ast}_{\beta}-\Gamma^{\ast}_{\gamma})g,D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

(5.82)

\displaystyle~~~+\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\left[\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)D^{2}_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})-\frac{3}{\alpha}\langle g,K\rangle_{\theta}D_{z}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma})),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle~~~-\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\langle\frac{3}{2\alpha}\langle D_{z}g,K\rangle_{\theta}(F^{\ast}_{\beta}-F^{\ast}_{\gamma}),D^{2}_{z}g(w^{\lambda})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\triangleq M_{31}+M_{32}+M_{33}.

Together with (
5.29
), it follows from a similar way as (
5.75
) that

\displaystyle|M_{31}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w_{\theta}^{\lambda}K^{\frac{\alpha}{10\beta}}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

(5.83)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{2}}\left(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\alpha(1-\eta)^{2}\|gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Along a similar way as (
5.76
), we deduce from (
5.29
) that

\displaystyle|M_{32}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}(\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}})\|w_{\theta}^{\lambda}K^{\frac{\alpha}{10\beta}}\|_{L^{2}_{\theta}}

(5.84)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{\frac{5}{2}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}(\|L^{-1}_{z,K}(g)w_{z}\|_{L^{2}_{z}}+\|gw^{K}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\left(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}\right).

Repeating the derivation scheme of (
5.77
) with (
5.29
) gives

\displaystyle|M_{33}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}

(5.85)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\left(\alpha(1-\eta)^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}gw^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1-\eta)^{2}\|D_{z}gw^{\eta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right).

Collecting the estimates from (
5.82
) through (
5.85
), we obtain that

\displaystyle|M_{3}|\lesssim\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\|g\|_{\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\eta}}^{2}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\beta^{3}}\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{-1}}.

(5.86)

By substituting (
5.80
) in
Step 2.1
, (
5.81
) in
Step 2.2
and (
5.86
) in
Step 2.3
into (
5.79
), then choosing the suitable transitional constant
\eta
,
\delta_{0}
, finally taking
\alpha
small enough, we directly derive (
5.56
).
Therefore, we finish the proof of this proposition.
∎

6
Elliptic estimates

In this section, we aim to establish elliptic estimates for the following boundary value problem across different Banach spaces:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}L^{\alpha}_{z}(\phi)+L_{\theta}(\phi)=f,\\[4.30554pt]
\phi|_{\partial D}=0,~~~\langle f,K\rangle_{\theta}=0,\end{array}\right.

where the domain
D\triangleq[0,\infty)\times[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
, the radial operator
L^{\alpha}_{z}(\phi)
is defined by

\displaystyle L^{\alpha}_{z}(\phi)=-\alpha^{2}D^{2}_{z}\phi-5\alpha D_{z}\phi,

(6.3)

and the angular operator
L_{\theta}(\phi)
is given by

\displaystyle L_{\theta}(\phi)=-\partial_{\theta}^{2}\phi+\partial_{\theta}\left(\tan\theta\phi\right)-6\phi.

(6.4)

Let
(L^{\alpha}_{z})^{\ast}
and
L^{*}_{\theta}
denote the adjoint operators of
L^{\alpha}_{z}
and
L_{\theta}
, respectively.
Moreover, for
\xi\in[0,\infty)
, we define the weight function:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\triangleq\mathcal{W}_{z}(z)\sin^{-\frac{\xi}{2}}(2\theta),

where
\mathcal{W}_{z}
denotes either
w_{z}
or
w_{z}^{\ast}
.
For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote that

\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}f\triangleq\sin^{n}(2\theta)\partial^{n}_{\theta}f.

It is easy to check that
\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}=D^{n}_{\theta}
for
n=0,1
.
We also define the weighted
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}(D)
-norm as follows:

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\triangleq\|f\mathcal{W}\|_{L^{2}}.

6.1.  Basic inequality

Our goal in this subsection is to present some fundamental inequalities and lemmas, which is useful for establishing the elliptic estimates.
We begin with some equalities of the trigonometric functions.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)K(\theta)d\theta=(-1)^{n}~\frac{4n}{(4n^{2}-1)(4n^{2}-9)}.

(6.5)

Proof.

By integration by parts, we readily check that for any
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(m\theta)\sin(n\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(m\theta)d\cos(n\theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{n\pi}{2}\right)+\frac{m}{n}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\cos(m\theta)\cos(n\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{n\pi}{2}\right)+\frac{m}{n^{2}}\cos\left(\frac{m\pi}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{n\pi}{2}\right)+\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(m\theta)\sin(n\theta)d\theta,

which implies that for any
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(m\theta)\sin(n\theta)d\theta=\frac{m}{n^{2}-m^{2}}\cos\left(\frac{m\pi}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{n\pi}{2}\right)-\frac{n}{n^{2}-m^{2}}\cos\left(\frac{n\pi}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{m\pi}{2}\right).

(6.6)

We recall the definition of
K(\theta)
in (
1.18
), then use integration by parts, to discover that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)K(\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta(\cos\theta)^{2}d\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta d\theta+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta\cos(2\theta)d\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta d\theta+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin(3\theta)d\theta-\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta(\cos\theta)^{2}d\theta.

This leads us to get that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)K(\theta)d\theta=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta d\theta+\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin(3\theta)d\theta.

(6.7)

By virtue of (
6.6
), one obtains that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin\theta d\theta+\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin(2n\theta)\sin(3\theta)d\theta=(-1)^{n}\frac{16n}{(4n^{2}-1)(4n^{2}-9)}.

Substituting the equality above into (
6.7
), we can get (
6.5
) directly.
∎

Then, we will derive some Hardy inequalities.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
\xi\in[0,\infty)
, assume that
f\in H^{n}([0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
and
f(0)=f(\frac{\pi}{2})=0
,
then there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2n}(2\theta)}d\theta\leq C\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}\left(\xi+2(n-j)-1\right)^{-2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial^{n}_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta.

(6.8)

Remark 6.3
.

While Lemma
7.3
in
[
5
]
established the Hardy inequality (
6.8
) for the specific case when
n=1
and
\xi=0
, we now present its extension to all positive integers
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and parameters
\xi\in[0,\infty)
in Lemma
6.2
.

The proof of Lemma
6.2
.

According to the density argument, we only need to prove that (
6.8
) holds for any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}([0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
.
And we will prove (
6.8
) by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
.

Suppose first
n=1
.
We divide the integration into the following two parts:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2}(2\theta)}d\theta=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2}(2\theta)}d\theta+\int_{\frac{\pi}{4}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2}(2\theta)}d\theta\triangleq\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2}.

(6.9)

Now, we will deal with
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
in turn.
It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\sin(2\theta)\geq\frac{4\theta}{\pi},\quad\text{for any}~~\theta\in\left[0,\frac{\pi}{4}\right].

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\theta^{\xi+2}}d\theta.

(6.10)

Integration by parts leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\theta^{\xi+2}}d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\xi+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}f^{2}d\theta^{-(\xi+1)}=-\frac{1}{\xi+1}\frac{f^{2}}{\theta^{\xi+1}}\bigg|^{\theta=\frac{\pi}{4}}_{\theta=0}+\frac{2}{\xi+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f\partial_{\theta}f}{\theta^{\xi+1}}d\theta\triangleq\mathcal{I}_{11}+\mathcal{I}_{12}.

(6.11)

For the first term
\mathcal{I}_{11}
, we deduce from
Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{11}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\frac{4}{\pi}\right)^{\xi+1}}{\xi+1}f^{2}\bigg|_{\theta=0}^{\theta=\frac{\pi}{4}}=-2\frac{\left(\frac{4}{\pi}\right)^{\xi+1}}{\xi+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}f\partial_{\theta}fd\theta\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\theta^{\xi+2}}d\theta+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\theta^{\xi}}d\theta,

where
\varepsilon>0
is a sufficiently small constant.
For another term
\mathcal{I}_{12}
, thanks to Cauchy-Schwartz inequality again, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{12}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\theta^{\xi+2}}d\theta+\frac{C_{\varepsilon}}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\theta^{\xi}}d\theta,

where
\varepsilon>0
is a sufficiently small constant.
We substitute the estimates of
\mathcal{I}_{11}
and
\mathcal{I}_{12}
into (
6.11
), choose
\varepsilon
suitably small, and then insert the resulting inequality into (
6.10
), to find that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\theta^{\xi}}d\theta\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta,

(6.12)

where we have used the fact that
2\theta\geq\sin(2\theta)
for any
\theta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{4}]
.
Regarding
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, it is not difficult to check that

\displaystyle\sin(2\theta)\geq\frac{4}{\pi}\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta\right),\quad 2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta\right)\geq\sin(2\theta),\quad\text{for any}~~\theta\in\left[\frac{\pi}{4},\frac{\pi}{2}\right].

Hence, by merging similar items in (
6.12
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}\lesssim\int_{\frac{\pi}{4}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta)^{\xi+2}}d\theta\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{\frac{\pi}{4}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta)^{\xi}}d\theta\lesssim\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{\frac{\pi}{4}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta.

(6.13)

Plugging (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) into (
6.9
), we get that (
6.8
) holds for
n=1
.

Next assume the estimate (
6.8
) is valid for all
1\leq n\leq l
.
Then for
n=l+1
, applying the induction assumption, we can deduce from a similar way as the case
n=1
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2(l+1)}(2\theta)}d\theta\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\xi+2(l+1)-1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2l}(2\theta)}d\theta\lesssim\prod_{j=0}^{l}\left(\xi+2(l-j)+1\right)^{-2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial^{l+1}_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta.

This implies that (
6.8
) holds for
n=l+1
.
We thus complete the proof by the standard induction argument.
∎

Similar calculations yield the following sharp version of Hardy inequality, which is optimal with respect to the order of the first constant on the right-hand side of (
6.14
) in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume
f\in H^{1}([0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
and
f(0)=f(\frac{\pi}{2})=0
, for any
\xi\in[0,\infty)
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2}(2\theta)}d\theta\leq\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta+C\|f\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

(6.14)

Remark 6.5
.

By comparing Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.4
, we see that the size of the first constant on the right-hand side of (
6.14
) is sharp.
In the following elliptic estimates in this section, we will make full use of the smallness of the first term, so that it can be absorbed by the main term.
We note that Corollary
7.7
in
[
5
]
established the result for the parameter range
\xi\in[0,1]
.
However, our analysis requires an extension to
\xi\in[0,2]
due to the angular weight
w_{\theta}^{\lambda}
in the norms our introduced norm
\mathcal{H}^{k}
,
\mathcal{H}^{k,\ast}
and
\mathcal{E}^{2}
, where
\lambda>1
by construction.
Remarkably, we can further generalize (
6.14
) to hold for all
\xi\in[0,+\infty)
.

The proof of Lemma
6.4
.

This proof is similar as that of Lemma
6.2
. Thus, we only prove the case that
\theta\in\left[0,\frac{\pi}{4}\right]
.
Let
\xi\geq 0
. Integration by parts leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi+2}}d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2(\xi+1)}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}f^{2}d(2\theta)^{-(\xi+1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2(\xi+1)}\frac{f^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi+1}}\bigg|^{\theta=\frac{\pi}{4}}_{\theta=0}+\frac{1}{\xi+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f\partial_{\theta}f}{(2\theta)^{\xi+1}}d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\xi+1}}{2(\xi+1)}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}2f\partial_{\theta}fd\theta+\frac{1}{\xi+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f\partial_{\theta}f}{(2\theta)^{\xi+1}}d\theta

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi+2}}d\theta+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi}}d\theta,

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi+2}}d\theta\leq\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{(2\theta)^{\xi}}d\theta+C\|f\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

(6.15)

For any
\xi\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{(2\theta)^{\xi}}-\frac{1}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}\right|\lesssim 1,\quad\text{for any}~~\theta\in\left[0,\frac{\pi}{4}\right].

This, together with (
6.15
), yields directly that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{f^{2}}{\sin^{\xi+2}(2\theta)}d\theta\leq\frac{1}{(\xi+1)^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{4}}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2}}{\sin^{\xi}(2\theta)}d\theta+C\|f\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

Thus, we finish the proof of this lemma.
∎

Moreover, based on the Hardy inequalities established in Lemma
8.26
of
[
5
]
and Lemma
6.2
, we can derive the following proposition.
For the sake of simplicity, the proof is omitted here.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
n\geq 0
. There holds

\displaystyle\|\frac{f}{\sin(2\theta)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{n}}\leq C\|\partial_{\theta}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{n}},\quad\|\frac{f}{\sin(2\theta)}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{n,\ast}}\leq C\|\partial_{\theta}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{n,\ast}}.

Our next goal is to investigate the equivalent norms of the operators
D_{\theta}
and
\tilde{D}_{\theta}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
n\geq 1
. There holds

\displaystyle\|D^{n}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}},\quad\|\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|D^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}.

(6.16)

Proof.

Recalling the definitions of
D^{n}_{\theta}
and
\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}
, it is easy to check that (
6.16
) holds for
n=1
.
For
n\geq 2
, there exists
\big\{S_{k}(\theta)\big\}_{k=1}^{n-1}\subset C^{\infty}([0,\frac{\pi}{2}])
such that

\displaystyle D^{n}_{\theta}f=\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}f+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}S_{k}(\theta)\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f,

(6.17)

which implies that

\displaystyle\|D^{n}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim\|\tilde{D}^{n}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\|S_{k}(\theta)\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{n}\|\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}.

This gives the first inequality in (
6.16
).

While for proving the second inequality in (
6.16
), we use the standard induction argument.
Suppose that the second inequality in (
6.16
) holds for any
0<n<l
, then for
n=l+1
, we deduce from (
6.17
) that

\displaystyle\tilde{D}^{l+1}_{\theta}f=D^{l+1}_{\theta}f-\sum_{k=1}^{l}S_{k}(\theta)\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f.

Combining this with the induction assumption, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{D}^{l+1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim\|D^{l+1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\sum_{k=1}^{l}\|S_{k}(\theta)\tilde{D}^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{l+1}\|D^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}.

Hence, the second inequality in (
6.16
) can be derive by the standard induction argument.
We thus complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

From now on, for any function
f
, we define the adjoint operators of
D_{\theta}
and
D_{z}
as follows:

D^{\ast}_{\theta}(f)\triangleq\partial_{\theta}(\sin(2\theta)f),\quad D^{\ast}_{z}(f)\triangleq\partial_{z}(zf).

The following lemma provides an estimate bounding the
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}
-norm of the mixed derivatives in terms of purely radial and angular derivatives.

Lemma 6.8
.

Assume that for any
k=1,2
,

\sum\limits_{l=0}^{k}\big|\frac{D^{l}_{z}D^{k-l}_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}\big|\leq C_{\beta}
, where
C_{\beta}>0
is a constant.
Then for any
i,j,n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
i+j=n
, there holds

\displaystyle\|D^{i}_{z}D^{j}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq C_{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\big(\|D^{k}_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\big).

(6.18)

Proof.

We will establish (
6.18
) by induction on
n
, the cases
n=0
and
n=1
follow directly.
For
n=2
, it is easy to check that (
6.18
) holds for
i=0
or
j=0
.
For
i=j=1
, one can deduce that

\displaystyle\|D_{z}D_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}

\displaystyle=-\langle D_{z}f,D_{z}D^{2}_{\theta}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle-\langle D_{z}f,D_{z}D_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{\theta}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle D^{2}_{z}f,D^{2}_{\theta}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle+\langle D_{z}f,D^{2}_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{z}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle-\langle D_{z}f,D_{z}D_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{\theta}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle D^{2}_{z}f,D^{2}_{\theta}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle+\langle D_{z}f,D^{2}_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{z}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle(D_{z}f)^{2},(D^{\ast}_{\theta})^{2}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\|D^{2}_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{2}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}},

which implies that (
6.18
) holds for
n=2
.
Assume that the following inequality is valid for
i+j\leq n
as:

\displaystyle\|D^{i}_{z}D^{j}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim_{\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{i+j}\big(\|D^{k}_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\big).

(6.19)

For
i+j=n+1
, (
6.18
) holds for
i=0
or
j=0
.
With the induction assumption (
6.19
), we deduce that for any
i+j=n+1
and
i,j\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\|D^{i}_{z}D^{j}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}

\displaystyle=-\langle D^{i}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f,D^{i}_{z}D^{j+1}_{\theta}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle-\langle D^{i}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f,D^{i}_{z}D^{j}_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{\theta}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

(6.20)

\displaystyle=\langle D^{i+1}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f,D^{i-1}_{z}D^{j+1}_{\theta}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle+\langle D^{i}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f,D^{i-1}_{z}D^{j+1}_{\theta}fD^{\ast}_{z}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{2}\langle(D^{i}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f)^{2},(D^{\ast}_{\theta})^{2}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\|D^{i+1}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{i-1}_{z}D^{j+1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{i}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\|D^{i+1}_{z}D^{j-1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{i-1}_{z}D^{j+1}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\|D^{k}_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\|D^{k}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\right).

This confirms that (
6.18
) is valid for
i=1
and
j=1
.
Performing the finite iterations, we can conclude that (
6.18
) for any
i+j=n+1
by similar calculations as (
6.20
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
6.8
.
∎

Furthermore, we can give the following lemma by similar argument as the proof of Lemma
6.8
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1]
. Assume that for any
k=1,2
,

\sum\limits_{l=0}^{k}\big|\frac{D^{l}_{z}D^{k-l}_{\theta}\mathcal{W}}{\mathcal{W}}\big|\leq C_{\beta}
,
where
C_{\beta}>0
is a constant.
Then there holds

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\|\partial_{\theta}D_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq C_{\beta}(\|\partial^{2}_{\theta}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\alpha^{4}\|D^{2}_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}).

Proof.

With the help of integrating by parts and Lemma
6.2
, we get that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\|\partial_{\theta}D_{z}f\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\langle\partial^{2}_{\theta}f,D^{2}_{z}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle+\alpha^{2}\langle\partial_{\theta}f,D^{2}_{z}f\partial_{\theta}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle-\alpha^{2}\langle\partial_{\theta}f,\partial_{\theta}D_{z}fD^{\ast}_{z}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\langle\partial^{2}_{\theta}f,D^{2}_{z}f\mathcal{W}^{2}\rangle+\alpha^{2}\langle\partial_{\theta}f,D^{2}_{z}f\partial_{\theta}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}\langle(\partial_{\theta}f)^{2},(D^{\ast}_{z})^{2}(\mathcal{W}^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta}\big\|\partial^{2}_{\theta}f\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\alpha^{4}\big\|D^{2}_{z}f\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}+\big\|\frac{\partial_{\theta}f}{\sin(2\theta)}\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathcal{W}}}
